\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nSDF MLP Decoder for A1 auxiliary task.\\\\n4-layer MLP, hidden=256, Softplus(beta=5), skip connection re-injecting xyz at layer 2.\\\\nValidated in KG-3 gradient tests.\\\\n\\\\nInput:\\\\n  features: [B, feature_dim] — mean-pooled suffix_out from action expert\\\\n  query_points: [B, N, 3] — 3D query positions in workspace coords\\\\n\\\\nOutput:\\\\n  sdf_values: [B, N] — predicted signed distance\\\\n  sdf_gradients: [B, N, 3] — spatial gradients (optional)\\\\n\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\\\n\\\\nimport torch\\\\nimport torch.nn as nn\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nclass SDFDecoder(nn.Module):\\\\n    def __init__(self, feature_dim: int = 1024, hidden_dim: int = 256):\\\\n        super().__init__()\\\\n        # Layer 0: concat(feature, xyz) -> hidden\\\\n        self.layer0 = nn.Linear(feature_dim + 3, hidden_dim)\\\\n        # Layer 1: hidden -> hidden\\\\n        self.layer1 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim)\\\\n        # Layer 2 (skip): hidden + xyz -> hidden\\\\n        self.layer2 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim + 3, hidden_dim)\\\\n        # Layer 3: hidden -> 1\\\\n        self.layer3 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1)\\\\n        self.activation = nn.Softplus(beta=5)\\\\n        self._init_weights()\\\\n\\\\n    def _init_weights(self) -> None:\\\\n        for m in self.modules():\\\\n            if isinstance(m, nn.Linear):\\\\n                nn.init.kaiming_normal_(m.weight, nonlinearity=\\\\\\\"relu\\\\\\\")\\\\n                if m.bias is not None:\\\\n                    nn.init.zeros_(m.bias)\\\\n\\\\n    def forward(\\\\n        self,\\\\n        features: torch.Tensor,\\\\n        query_points: torch.Tensor,\\\\n        return_gradients: bool = False,\\\\n    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor | None]:\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        Args:\\\\n            features: [B, feature_dim] mean-pooled action expert features\\\\n            query_points: [B, N, 3]\\\\n            return_gradients: compute spatial gradients for Eikonal loss\\\\n\\\\n        Returns:\\\\n            sdf: [B, N]\\\\n            grad_sdf: [B, N, 3] or None\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        B, N, _ = query_points.shape\\\\n\\\\n        if return_gradients:\\\\n            xyz = query_points.detach().requires_grad_(True)\\\\n        else:\\\\n            xyz = query_points\\\\n\\\\n        feat = features.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, N, -1)  # [B, N, feat_dim]\\\\n        x = torch.cat([feat, xyz], dim=-1)  # [B, N, feat_dim+3]\\\\n\\\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer0(x))\\\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer1(h))\\\\n        h = torch.cat([h, xyz], dim=-1)  # skip: re-inject xyz\\\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer2(h))\\\\n        sdf = self.layer3(h).squeeze(-1)  # [B, N]\\\\n\\\\n        if return_gradients:\\\\n            grad_outputs = torch.ones_like(sdf)\\\\n            (grad_sdf,) = torch.autograd.grad(\\\\n                outputs=sdf,\\\\n                inputs=xyz,\\\\n                grad_outputs=grad_outputs,\\\\n                create_graph=self.training,\\\\n                retain_graph=True,\\\\n            )\\\\n            return sdf, grad_sdf\\\\n\\\\n        return sdf, None\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef compute_sdf_loss(\\\\n    sdf_pred: torch.Tensor,\\\\n    sdf_gt: torch.Tensor,\\\\n    sdf_gradients: torch.Tensor | None,\\\\n    clamp_delta: float = 0.05,\\\\n    eikonal_weight: float = 0.1,\\\\n) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Clamped L1 + Eikonal regularization.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    pred_c = sdf_pred.clamp(-clamp_delta, clamp_delta)\\\\n    gt_c = sdf_gt.clamp(-clamp_delta, clamp_delta)\\\\n    sdf_l1 = (pred_c - gt_c).abs().mean()\\\\n\\\\n    losses = {\\\\\\\"sdf_l1\\\\\\\": sdf_l1}\\\\n\\\\n    if sdf_gradients is not None:\\\\n        grad_norm = sdf_gradients.norm(dim=-1)\\\\n        eikonal = ((grad_norm - 1.0) ** 2).mean()\\\\n        losses[\\\\\\\"eikonal\\\\\\\"] = eikonal\\\\n        losses[\\\\\\\"sdf_total\\\\\\\"] = sdf_l1 + eikonal_weight * eikonal\\\\n    else:\\\\n        losses[\\\\\\\"sdf_total\\\\\\\"] = sdf_l1\\\\n\\\\n    return losses\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.51}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"36461722-2e57-4b61-892b-43cb09f5000b","uuid":"90e688a9-2ae9-4fbe-9c4f-8173b491e690","timestamp":"2026-05-19T21:07:56.964Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": feat_dim+3]\\\\n\\\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer0(x))\\\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer1(h))\\\\n        h = torch.cat([h, xyz], dim=-1)  # skip: re-inject xyz\\\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer2(h))\\\\n        sdf = self.layer3(h).squeeze(-1)  # [B, N]\\\\n\\\\n        if return_gradients:\\\\n            grad_outputs = torch.ones_like(sdf)\\\\n               retain_graph=True,\\\\n            )\\\\n            return sdf, grad_sdf\\\\n\\\\n        return sdf, None\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef compute_sdf_loss(\\\\n    sdf_pred: torch.Tensor,\\\\n    sdf_gt: torch.Tensor,\\\\n    sdf_gradients: torch.Tensor | None,\\\\n    clamp_delta: float = 0.05,\\\\n    eikonal_weight: float = 0.1,\\\\n) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Clamped L1 + Eikonal regularization.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    pred_c = sdf_pred.clamp(-clamp_delta, clamp_delta)\\\\n    gt_c = sdf_gt.clamp(-clamp_delta, clamp_delta)\\\\n    sdf_l1 = (pred_c - gt_c).abs().mean()\\\\n\\\\n    losses = {\\\\\\\"sdf_l1\\\\\\\": sdf_l1}\\\\n\\\\n    if sdf_gradients is not None:\\\\n        grad_norm = sdf_gradients.norm(dim=-1)\\\\n        eikonal = ((grad_norm - 1.0) ** 2).mean()\\\\n        losses[\\\\\\\"eikonal\\\\\\\"] = eikonal\\\\n        losses[\\\\\\\"sdf_total\\\\\\\"] = sdf_l1 + eikonal_weight * eikonal\\\\n    else:\\\\n        losses[\\\\\\\"sdf_total\\\\\\\"] = sdf_l1\\\\n\\\\n    return losses\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.51}}\"}","structuredContent":{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nSDF MLP Decoder for A1 auxiliary task.\\n4-layer MLP, hidden=256, Softplus(beta=5), skip connection re-injecting xyz at layer 2.\\nValidated in KG-3 gradient tests.\\n\\nInput:\\n  features: [B, feature_dim] — mean-pooled suffix_out from action expert\\n  query_points: [B, N, 3] — 3D query positions in workspace coords\\n\\nOutput:\\n  sdf_values: [B, N] — predicted signed distance\\n  sdf_gradients: [B, N, 3] — spatial gradients (optional)\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport torch\\nimport torch.nn as nn\\n\\n\\nclass SDFDecoder(nn.Module):\\n    def __init__(self, feature_dim: int = 1024, hidden_dim: int = 256):\\n        super().__init__()\\n        # Layer 0: concat(feature, xyz) -> hidden\\n        self.layer0 = nn.Linear(feature_dim + 3, hidden_dim)\\n        # Layer 1: hidden -> hidden\\n        self.layer1 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim)\\n        # Layer 2 (skip): hidden + xyz -> hidden\\n        self.layer2 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim + 3, hidden_dim)\\n        # Layer 3: hidden -> 1\\n        self.layer3 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, 1)\\n        self.activation = nn.Softplus(beta=5)\\n        self._init_weights()\\n\\n    def _init_weights(self) -> None:\\n        for m in self.modules():\\n            if isinstance(m, nn.Linear):\\n                nn.init.kaiming_normal_(m.weight, nonlinearity=\\\"relu\\\")\\n                if m.bias is not None:\\n                    nn.init.zeros_(m.bias)\\n\\n    def forward(\\n        self,\\n        features: torch.Tensor,\\n        query_points: torch.Tensor,\\n        return_gradients: bool = False,\\n    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor | None]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Args:\\n            features: [B, feature_dim] mean-pooled action expert features\\n            query_points: [B, N, 3]\\n            return_gradients: compute spatial gradients for Eikonal loss\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            sdf: [B, N]\\n            grad_sdf: [B, N, 3] or None\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        B, N, _ = query_points.shape\\n\\n        if return_gradients:\\n            xyz = query_points.detach().requires_grad_(True)\\n        else:\\n            xyz = query_points\\n\\n        feat = features.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, N, -1)  # [B, N, feat_dim]\\n        x = torch.cat([feat, xyz], dim=-1)  # [B, N, feat_dim+3]\\n\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer0(x))\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer1(h))\\n        h = torch.cat([h, xyz], dim=-1)  # skip: re-inject xyz\\n        h = self.activation(self.layer2(h))\\n        sdf = self.layer3(h).squeeze(-1)  # [B, N]\\n\\n        if return_gradients:\\n            grad_outputs = torch.ones_like(sdf)\\n            (grad_sdf,) = torch.autograd.grad(\\n                outputs=sdf,\\n                inputs=xyz,\\n                grad_outputs=grad_outputs,\\n                create_graph=self.training,\\n                retain_graph=True,\\n            )\\n            return sdf, grad_sdf\\n\\n        return sdf, None\\n\\n\\ndef compute_sdf_loss(\\n    sdf_pred: torch.Tensor,\\n    sdf_gt: torch.Tensor,\\n    sdf_gradients: torch.Tensor | None,\\n    clamp_delta: float = 0.05,\\n    eikonal_weight: float = 0.1,\\n) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Clamped L1 + Eikonal regularization.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    pred_c = sdf_pred.clamp(-clamp_delta, clamp_delta)\\n    gt_c = sdf_gt.clamp(-clamp_delta, clamp_delta)\\n    sdf_l1 = (pred_c - gt_c).abs().mean()\\n\\n    losses = {\\\"sdf_l1\\\": sdf_l1}\\n\\n    if sdf_gradients is not None:\\n        grad_norm = sdf_gradients.norm(dim=-1)\\n        eikonal = ((grad_norm - 1.0) ** 2).mean()\\n        losses[\\\"eikonal\\\"] = eikonal\\n        losses[\\\"sdf_total\\\"] = sdf_l1 + eikonal_weight * eikonal\\n    else:\\n        losses[\\\"sdf_total\\\"] = sdf_l1\\n\\n    return losses\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"}}}

Use validation to find the latest working checkpoint\\\\n            latest_step = get_latest_checkpoint_step(exp_checkpoint_dir)\\\\n            if latest_step is not None:\\\\n                resuming = True\\\\n                logging.info(\\\\n                    f\\\\\\\"Resuming from experiment checkpoint directory: {exp_checkpoint_dir} at step {latest_step}\\\\\\\"\\\\n                )\\\\n            else:\\\\n                raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\\\\\"No valid checkpoints found in {exp_checkpoint_dir} for resume\\\\\\\")\\\\n        else:\\\\n            raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\\\\\"Experiment checkpoint directory {exp_checkpoint_dir} does not exist for resume\\\\\\\")\\\\n    elif config.overwrite and config.checkpoint_dir.exists():\\\\n        shutil.rmtree(config.checkpoint_dir)\\\\n        logging.info(f\\\\\\\"Overwriting checkpoint directory: {config.checkpoint_dir}\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    # Create checkpoint directory with experiment name\\\\n    if not resuming:\\\\n        # For new runs, create experiment-specific checkpoint directory\\\\n        exp_checkpoint_dir = config.checkpoint_dir\\\\n        exp_checkpoint_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\\\n        logging.info(f\\\\\\\"Created experiment checkpoint directory: {exp_checkpoint_dir}\\\\\\\")\\\\n    else:\\\\n        # For resume, checkpoint_dir is already set to the experiment directory\\\\n        logging.info(f\\\\\\\"Using existing experiment checkpoint directory: {config.checkpoint_dir}\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    # Initialize wandb (only on main process)\\\\n    if is_main:\\\\n        init_wandb(config, resuming=resuming, enabled=config.wandb_enabled)\\\\n\\\\n    # Build data loader using the unified data loader\\\\n    # Calculate effective batch size per GPU for DDP\\\\n    # For N GPUs, each GPU should get batch_size/N samples, so total across all GPUs is batch_size\\\\n    world_size = torch.distributed.get_world_size() if use_ddp else 1\\\\n    effective_batch_size = config.batch_size // world_size\\\\n    logging.info(\\\\n        f\\\\\\\"Using batch size per GPU: {effective_batch_size} (total batch size across {world_size} GPUs: {config.batch_size})\\\\\\\"\\\\n    )\\\\n\\\\n    # Pass the original batch size to data loader - it will handle DDP splitting internally\\\\n    loader, data_config = build_datasets(config)\\\\n\\\\n    # Log sample images to wandb on first batch\\\\n    if is_main and config.wandb_enabled and not resuming:\\\\n        # Create a separate data loader for sample batch to avoid consuming the main loader\\\\n        sample_data_loader = _data.create_data_loader(config, framework=\\\\\\\"pytorch\\\\\\\", shuffle=False)\\\\n        sample_batch = next(iter(sample_data_loader))\\\\n        # Convert observation and actions to torch tensors\\\\n        observation, actions = sample_batch\\\\n        sample_batch = observation.to_dict()\\\\n        sample_batch[\\\\\\\"actions\\\\\\\"] = actions\\\\n\\\\n        # Create sample images for wandb\\\\n        images_to_log = []\\\\n        # Get batch size from the first image tensor\\\\n        batch_size = next(iter(sample_batch[\\\\\\\"image\\\\\\\"].values())).shape[0]\\\\n        for i in range(min(5, batch_size)):\\\\n            # Concatenate all camera views horizontally for this batch item\\\\n            # Convert from NCHW to NHWC format for wandb\\\\n            img_concatenated = torch.cat([img[i].permute(1, 2, 0) for img in sample_batch[\\\\\\\"image\\\\\\\"].values()], axis=1)\\\\n            img_concatenated = img_concatenated.cpu().numpy()\\\\n            images_to_log.append(wandb.Image(img_concatenated))\\\\n\\\\n        wandb.log({\\\\\\\"camera_views\\\\\\\": images_to_log}, step=0)\\\\n\\\\n        # Clear sample batch from memory aggressively\\\\n        del sample_batch, observation, actions, images_to_log, img_concatenated\\\\n        del sample_data_loader  # Also delete the sample data loader\\\\n        gc.collect()\\\\n        i\\\\n\\\\n... Check param AFTER loading\\\\n        test_p = dict(_m.named_parameters())['action_in_proj.weight']\\\\n        print(f\\\\\\\"DIAG: AFTER load - action_in_proj.weight device={test_p.device}, mean={test_p.data.float().mean().item():.6f}, std={test_p.data.float().std().item():.6f}\\\\\\\", flush=True)\\\\n        \\\\n        # Optimizer + learning rate schedule from config\\\\n    warmup_steps = config.lr_schedule.warmup_steps\\\\n    peak_lr = config.lr_schedule.peak_lr\\\\n    decay_steps = config.lr_schedule.decay_steps\\\\n    end_lr = config.lr_schedule.decay_lr\\\\n\\\\n    # Create optimizer with config parameters\\\\n    optim = torch.optim.AdamW(\\\\n        model.parameters(),\\\\n        lr=peak_lr,\\\\n        betas=(config.optimizer.b1, config.optimizer.b2),\\\\n        eps=config.optimizer.eps,\\\\n        weight_decay=config.optimizer.weight_decay,\\\\n    )\\\\n\\\\n    # \\\"import json\\\\nimport pathlib\\\\n\\\\nimport numpy as np\\\\nimport numpydantic\\\\nimport pydantic\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n@pydantic.dataclasses.dataclass\\\\nclass NormStats:\\\\n    mean: numpydantic.NDArray\\\\n    std: numpydantic.NDArray\\\\n    q01: numpydantic.NDArray | None = None  # 1st quantile\\\\n    q99: numpydantic.NDArray | None = None  # 99th quantile\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nclass RunningStats:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Compute running statistics of a batch of vectors.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    def __init__(self):\\\\n        self._count = 0\\\\n        self._mean = None\\\\n        self._mean_of_squares = None\\\\n        self._min = None\\\\n        self._max = None\\\\n        self._histograms = None\\\\n        self._bin_edges = None\\\\n        self._num_quantile_bins = 5000  # for computing quantiles on the fly\\\\n\\\\n    def update(self, batch: np.ndarray) -> None:\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        Update the running statistics with a batch of vectors.\\\\n\\\\n        Args:\\\\n            vectors (np.ndarray): An array where all dimensions except the last are batch dimensions.\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        batch = batch.reshape(-1, batch.shape[-1])\\\\n        num_elements, vector_length = batch.shape\\\\n        if self._count == 0:\\\\n            self._mean = np.mean(batch, axis=0)\\\\n            self._mean_of_squares = np.mean(batch**2, axis=0)\\\\n            self._min = np.min(batch, axis=0)\\\\n            self._max = np.max(batch, axis=0)\\\\n            self._histograms = [np.zeros(self._num_quantile_bins) for _ in range(vector_length)]\\\\n            self._bin_edges = [\\\\n                np.linspace(self._min[i] - 1e-10, self._max[i] + 1e-10, self._num_quantile_bins + 1)\\\\n                for i in range(vector_length)\\\\n            ]\\\\n        else:\\\\n            if vector_length != self._mean.size:\\\\n                raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"The length of new vectors does not match the initialized vector length.\\\\\\\")\\\\n            new_max = np.max(batch, axis=0)\\\\n            new_min = np.min(batch, axis=0)\\\\n            max_changed = np.any(new_max > self._max)\\\\n            min_changed = np.any(new_min < self._min)\\\\n            self._max = np.maximum(self._max, new_max)\\\\n            self._min = np.minimum(self._min, new_min)\\\\n\\\\n            if max_changed or min_changed:\\\\n                self._adjust_histograms()\\\\n\\\\n        self._count += num_elements\\\\n\\\\n        batch_mean = np.mean(batch, axis=0)\\\\n        batch_mean_of_squares = np.mean(batch**2, axis=0)\\\\n\\\\n        # Update running mean and mean of squares.\\\\n        self._mean += (batch_mean - self._mean) * (num_elements / self._count)\\\\n        self._mean_of_squares += (batch_mean_of_squares - self._mean_of_squares) * (num_elements / self._count)\\\\n\\\\n        self._update_histograms(batch)\\\\n\\\\n    def get_statistics(self) -> NormStats:\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        Compute and return the statistics of the vectors processed so far.\\\\n\\\\n        Returns:\\\\n            dict: A dictionary containing the computed statistics.\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        if self._count < 2:\\\\n            raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"Cannot compute statistics for less than 2 vectors.\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n        variance = self._mean_of_squares - self._mean**2\\\\n        stddev = np.sqrt(np.maximum(0, variance))\\\\n        q01, q99 = self._compute_quantiles([0.01, 0.99])\\\\n        return NormStats(mean=self._mean, std=stddev, q01=q01, q99=q99)\\\\n\\\\n    def _adjust_histograms(self):\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Adjust histograms when min or max changes.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        for i in range(len(self._histograms)):\\\\n            old_edges = self._bin_edges[i]\\\\n            new_edges = np.linspace(self._min[i], self._max[i], self._num_quantile_bins + 1)\\\\n\\\\n            # Redistribute the existing histogram counts to the new bins\\\\n            new_hist, _ = np.histogram(old_edges[:-1], bins=new_edges, weights=self._histograms[i])\\\\n\\\\n            self._histograms[i] = new_hist\\\\n            self._bin_edges[i] = new_edges\\\\n\\\\n    def _update_histograms(self, batch: np.ndarray) -> None:\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Update histograms with new vectors.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        for i in range(batch.shape[1]):\\\\n            hist, _ = np.histogram(batch[:, i], bins=self._bin_edges[i])\\\\n            self._histograms[i] += hist\\\\n\\\\n    def _compute_quantiles(self, quantiles):\\\\n        \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Compute quantiles based on histograms.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n        results = []\\\\n        for q in quantiles:\\\\n            target_count = q * self._count\\\\n            q_values = []\\\\n            for hist, edges in zip(self._histograms, self._bin_edges, strict=True):\\\\n                cumsum = np.cumsum(hist)\\\\n                idx = np.searchsorted(cumsum, target_count)\\\\n                q_values.append(edges[idx])\\\\n            results.append(np.array(q_values))\\\\n        return results\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nclass _NormStatsDict(pydantic.BaseModel):\\\\n    norm_stats: dict[str, NormStats]\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef serialize_json(norm_stats: dict[str, NormStats]) -> str:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Serialize the running statistics to a JSON string.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    return _NormStatsDict(norm_stats=norm_stats).model_dump_json(indent=2)\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef deserialize_json(data: str) -> dict[str, NormStats]:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Deserialize the running statistics from a JSON string.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    return _NormStatsDict(**json.loads(data)).norm_stats\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef save(directory: pathlib.Path | str, norm_stats: dict[str, NormStats]) -> None:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Save the normalization stats to a directory.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    path = pathlib.Path(directory) / \\\\\\\"norm_stats.json\\\\\\\"\\\\n    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\\\n    path.write_text(serialize_json(norm_stats))\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef load(directory: pathlib.Path | str) -> dict[str, NormStats]:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Load the normalization stats from a directory.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    path = pathlib.Path(directory) / \\\\\\\"norm_stats.json\\\\\\\"\\\\n    if not path.exists():\\\\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\\\\\"Norm stats file The `norm_stats` come from `data_config.norm_stats`, which is computed separately using `scripts/compute_norm_stats.py`.\n\n2. **freeze_filter**: Is set in config as an NNX filter. But the train_pytorch.py code **never uses it**! Looking at the train loop, there's no code that applies freeze_filter to set requires_grad=False. \n\nThis means ALL parameters are being trained, not just LoRA parameters. But seed 0 works fine with loss=0.22, so either the LoRA variant has fewer total parameters, or all 3.5B params are being fine-tuned. Let me check what the difference is between \"gemma_2b_lora\" and \"gemma_2b\" in the Pi0Config model variants.\n\n3. **suffix_out**: The model's forward() returns suffix_out (the action expert hidden states), which gets projected through `action_out_proj` to produce the velocity prediction.\n\nFor the SDF decoder, I'd need to extract features from this suffix_out tensor at shape `[B, action_horizon, hidden_dim]`. The real puzzle is why seed 1 has loss=177 while seed 0 has loss=0.22. Both show the same JAX and wandb warnings at startup with no DIAG lines, so the issue likely stems from seed 1 running with an older version of train_pytorch.py that didn't load weights correctly, or there's a difference in how they were initialized from their respective log directories.\n\nLooking at the startup paths more carefully—seed 0 came from `/tmp/train_spatial.log` while seed 1 came from `/root/autodl-tmp/logs/baseline_spatial_bowl_seed1.log`—I need to verify whether seed 0 was also started via train_baseline.py. The script sets `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` based on the `--gpu` argument, so both should map to cuda:0 after that assignment, but maybe seed 0 wasn't initialized the same way.\n\nThe loss of 177 is most likely caused by either missing weight loading (leaving the model with random initialization) or unnormalized actions producing very large values—I should check the git history of train_pytorch.py to see if there was a bug when seed 1 started, and verify whether the weights were actually being loaded at that {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01NNzkCtawLNYvbSsPKSaqDj","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Now I have a much clearer picture. Let me summarize:\n\n### Key Findings:\n\n**1. Model Forward Pass (pi0_pytorch.py lines 317-376)**:\n- `forward()` computes the MSE loss between `u_t = noise - actions` and `v_t` (model prediction)\n- `suffix_out` is the output of the action expert, shape `[B, action_horizon, hidden_dim]`\n- `suffix_out` goes through `action_out_proj` to produce `v_t`\n- The loss is `F.mse_loss(u_t, v_t, reduction=\"none\")` — per-element MSE\n\n\n- Normalization happens upstream in the data loader pipeline via the `Normalize` transform, which applies statistics computed by a separate script\n- The freeze filter in `Pi0Config` uses a regex pattern to identify and freeze all parameters except those containing \"lora\" in their path names\n- However, `train_pytorch.py` bypasses this entirely by training all model parameters without ever setting `requires_grad=False`, so it's doing full fine-tuning despite the LoRA architecture\n- The seed discrepancy (Seed 0 loss=0.22 vs Seed 1 loss=177) appears to stem from different training scripts being used at different times, with Seed 0 benefiting from pre-trained weights and a very low initial learning rate\n\nNow I'm reconsidering the file timestamps—the 04:52 \"birth\" time for `train_pytorch.py` likely indicates when the inode was created through a file replacement operation, not when the file first appeared. Since both training runs started before that timestamp, they must have imported an earlier version of the file. The `object_pudding` log showing loss=536 suggests it's a completely different training scenario with different data, which would explain the dramatically higher loss values.\n\nThe pattern across all three runs—`object_pudding` at 536, `spatial_bowl` seed 1 at 177, and seed 0 at 0.22—points to random weight initialization rather than loading a pre-trained checkpoint. This 800x difference in loss is consistent with what you'd expect from a randomly initialized 3.5B model producing essentially random predictions. The most likely explanation is that seed 0 started with a different version of `train_pytorch.py` that included weight loading, while seed 1 and `object_pudding` ran after the file was modified to remove that functionality.\n\nSince seed 0 was started by an integrated Worker mechanism (with logs at `/tmp/train_spatial.log`) rather than through `train_baseline.py`, it probably used a different initialization path—possibly calling `train_pytorch.py` directly with a config name instead of going through the baseline script. The quickest way to verify what's actually happening is to run a test directly on the server to see which version of the code is currently active.\n\nBefore I do that, I need to understand what This confirms the A1 Worker's earlier report — \"PyTorch pi0 无 LoRA\" is correct.\n\n2. **freeze_filter**: The `get_freeze_filter()` in `pi0_config.py` is a JAX NNX filter. It uses `nnx.Not(nnx_utils.PathRegex(\".*lora.*\"))` which is JAX NNX specific and won't work in PyTorch.\n   \n3. **Weight file**: The base weights exist at `/root/autodl-tmp/openpi_weights/pi0_base/model.safetensors` along with supporting config files. `paligemma_variant=\"gemma_2b_lora\"` which creates a mismatch.\n\nNow I'm wondering what actually happens when the PyTorch model gets initialized with a config that specifies a LoRA variant but the model itself doesn't implement LoRA — I need to trace through how `paligemma_variant` is actually used in the PyTorch action_expert={model_config.action_expert_variant}\")\n\nmodel = pi0_pytorch.PI0Pytorch(model_config).to(\"cuda:0\")\ntotal_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())\ntrainable_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad)\nprint(f\"Total params: {total_params:,}\")\nprint(f\"Trainable params: {trainable_params:,}\")\nprint(f\"All trainable: {total_params == trainable_params}\")\n\n# Check a param BEFORE loading\ntest_p = dict(model.named_parameters())['action_in_proj.weight']\nprint(f\"\\nBEFORE load - action_in_proj.weight: mean={test_p.data.float().mean():.6f}, std={test_p.data.float().std():.6f}\")\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\nprint(\"PART 3: Load weights\")\nprint(\"=\" * 60)\n\nweight_path = \"/root/autodl-tmp/openpi_weights/pi0_base\"\nmodel_path = os.path.join(weight_path, \"model.safetensors\")\nprint(f\"Weight path: {model_path}, exists: {os.path.exists(model_path)}\")\n\nmissing, unexpected = safetensors.torch.load_model(model, model_path)\nprint(f\"Missing keys: {len(missing)}\")\nprint(f\"Unexpected keys: {len(unexpected)}\")\nif missing:\n    print(f\"  Missing: {missing[:5]}...\")\nif unexpected:\n    print(f\"  Unexpected: {unexpected[:5]}...\")\n\n# Check a param AFTER loading  \ntest_p = dict(model.named_parameters())['action_in_proj.weight']\nprint(f\"\\nAFTER load - action_in_proj.weight: mean={test_p.data.float().mean():.6f}, std={test_p.data.float().std():.6f}\")\n\n# Compare with reference from file\nfrom safetensors import safe_open\nwith safe_open(model_path, framework='pt') as f:\n    ref = f.get_tensor('action_in_proj.weight')\n    print(f\"REF from file: mean={ref.float().mean():.6f}, std={ref.float().std():.6f}\")\n    match = torch.allclose(test_p.data.cpu().float(), ref.float(), atol=1e-5)\n    print(f\"Weights match reference: {match}\")\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\nprint(\"PART 4: Check norm_stats (data pipeline)\")\nprint(\"=\" * 60)\n\nimport openpi.training.config as _config\nfrom openpi.training.optimizer import CosineDecaySchedule\nimport openpi.training.data_loader as _data\n\nconfig = _config.TrainConfig(\n    name=\"baseline_spatial_bowl\",\n    project_name=\"colliforce-vla\",\n    exp_name=\"verify_test\",\n    model=model_config,\n    data=_config.LeRobotLiberoDataConfig(\n        repo_id=\"safelibero_spatial\",\n        base_config=_config.DataConfig(prompt_from_task=True),\n checkpoint_base_dir=\"/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints\",\n    seed=0,\n    wandb_enabled=False,\n    overwrite=True,\n)\nprint(f\"pytorch_weight_path in config: {config.pytorch_weight_path}\")\n\n# Build data loader\nloader = _data.create_data_loader(config, framework=\"pytorch\", shuffle=True)\ndata_config = loader.data_config()\nprint(f\"data_config type: {type(data_config)}\")\nif hasattr(data_config, 'norm_stats') and data_config.norm_stats:\n    print(f\"norm_stats keys: {list(data_config.norm_stats.keys())}\")\n    for k, v in data_config.norm_stats.items():\n        print(f\"  {k}: mean shape={v.mean.shape}, std shape={v.std.shape}\")\nelse:\n    print(\"WARNING: norm_stats is None or empty!\")\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\nprint(\"PART 5: Forward pass loss check\")\nprint(\"=\" * 60)\n\nmodel.train()\nbatch = next(iter(loader))\nimport jax\nobservation, actions = batch\nobservation = jax.tree.map(lambda x: x.to(\"cuda:0\"), observation)\nactions = actions.to(torch.float32).to(\"cuda:0\")\n\nwith torch.no_grad():\n    losses = model(observation, actions)\n    loss = losses.mean()\n    print(f\"Initial loss (with loaded weights): {loss.item():.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Expected: ~0.2 (if weights loaded correctly)\")\n    print(f\"If ~100+, weights were NOT loaded properly\")\n\n# Also test with random weights\nmodel2 = {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YW2JD3e8d6DEzgjhVMumsd","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"import logging\\\\nimport math\\\\n\\\\nimport torch\\\\nfrom torch import Tensor\\\\nfrom torch import nn\\\\nimport torch.nn.functional as F  # noqa: N812\\\\n\\\\nimport openpi.models.gemma as _gemma\\\\nfrom openpi.models_pytorch.gemma_pytorch import PaliGemmaWithExpertModel\\\\nimport openpi.models_pytorch.preprocessing_pytorch as _preprocessing\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef get_safe_dtype(target_dtype, device_type):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Get a safe dtype for the given device type.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    if device_type == \\\\\\\"cpu\\\\\\\":\\\\n        # CPU doesn't support bfloat16, use float32 instead\\\\n        if target_dtype == torch.bfloat16:\\\\n            return torch.float32\\\\n        if target_dtype == torch.float64:\\\\n            return torch.float64\\\\n    return target_dtype\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef create_sinusoidal_pos_embedding(\\\\n    time: torch.tensor, dimension: int, min_period: float, max_period: float, device=\\\\\\\"cpu\\\\\\\"\\\\n) -> Tensor:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Computes sine-cosine positional embedding vectors for scalar positions.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    if dimension % 2 != 0:\\\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\"dimension ({dimension}) must be divisible by 2\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    if time.ndim != 1:\\\\n        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"The time tensor is expected to be of shape `(batch_size, )`.\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    dtype = get_safe_dtype(torch.float64, device.type)\\\\n    fraction = torch.linspace(0.0, 1.0, dimension // 2, dtype=dtype, device=device)\\\\n    period = min_period * (max_period / min_period) ** fraction\\\\n\\\\n    # Compute the outer product\\\\n    scaling_factor = 1.0 / period * 2 * math.pi\\\\n    sin_input = scaling_factor[None, :] * time[:, None]\\\\n    return torch.cat([torch.sin(sin_input), torch.cos(sin_input)], dim=1)\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef sample_beta(alpha, beta, bsize, device):\\\\n    alpha_t = torch.as_tensor(alpha, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\\\n    beta_t = torch.as_tensor(beta, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\\\n    dist = torch.distributions.Beta(alpha_t, beta_t)\\\\n    return dist.sample((bsize,))\\\\n\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"36461722-2e57-4b61-892b-43cb09f5000b","uuid":"5d09ef5f-2d3f-4ac6-9cd0-20a5e44a676d","timestamp":"2026-05-19T21:14:51.008Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"import logging\\\\nimport math\\\\n\\\\nimport torch\\\\nfrom torch import Tensor\\\\nfrom torch import nn\\\\nimport torch.nn.functional as F  # CPU doesn't support bfloat16, use float32 instead\\\\n        if target_dtype == torch.bfloat16:\\\\n            return torch.float32\\\\n        if target_dtype == torch.float64:\\\\n            return torch.float64\\\\n    return target_dtype\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef create_sinusoidal_pos_embedding(\\\\n    time: torch.tensor, dimension: int, min_period: float, max_period: float, device=\\\\\\\"cpu\\\\\\\"\\\\n) -> Tensor:\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Computes sine-cosine positional embedding vectors for scalar positions.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    if dimension % 2 != 0:\\\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\"dimension ({dimension}) must be divisible by 2\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    if time.ndim != 1:\\\\n        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\"The time tensor is expected to be of shape `(batch_size, )`.\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    dtype = get_safe_dtype(torch.float64, device.type)\\\\n    fraction = torch.linspace(0.0, 1.0, dimension // 2, dtype=dtype, device=device)\\\\n    period = min_period * (max_period / min_period) ** fraction\\\\n\\\\n    # Compute the outer product\\\\n    scaling_factor = 1.0 / period * 2 * math.pi\\\\n    sin_input = scaling_factor[None, :] * time[:, None]\\\\n    return torch.cat([torch.sin(sin_input), torch.cos(sin_input)], dim=1)\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef sample_beta(alpha, beta, bsize, device):\\\\n    alpha_t = torch.as_tensor(alpha, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\\\n    beta_t = torch.as_tensor(beta, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\\\n    dist = torch.distributions.Beta(alpha_t, beta_t)\\\\n    return dist.sample((bsize,))\\\\n\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}","structuredContent":{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"import logging\\nimport math\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom torch import Tensor\\nfrom torch import nn\\nimport torch.nn.functional as F  # noqa: N812\\n\\nimport openpi.models.gemma as _gemma\\nfrom openpi.models_pytorch.gemma_pytorch import PaliGemmaWithExpertModel\\nimport openpi.models_pytorch.preprocessing_pytorch as _preprocessing\\n\\n\\ndef get_safe_dtype(target_dtype, device_type):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Get a safe dtype for the given device type.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if device_type == \\\"cpu\\\":\\n        # CPU doesn't support bfloat16, use float32 instead\\n        if target_dtype == torch.bfloat16:\\n            return torch.float32\\n        if target_dtype == torch.float64:\\n            return torch.float64\\n    return target_dtype\\n\\n\\ndef create_sinusoidal_pos_embedding(\\n    time: torch.tensor, dimension: int, min_period: float, max_period: float, device=\\\"cpu\\\"\\n) -> Tensor:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Computes sine-cosine positional embedding vectors for scalar positions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if dimension % 2 != 0:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"dimension ({dimension}) must be divisible by 2\\\")\\n\\n    if time.ndim != 1:\\n        raise ValueError(\\\"The time tensor is expected to be of shape `(batch_size, )`.\\\")\\n\\n    dtype = get_safe_dtype(torch.float64, device.type)\\n    fraction = torch.linspace(0.0, 1.0, dimension // 2, dtype=dtype, device=device)\\n    period = min_period * (max_period / min_period) ** fraction\\n\\n    # Compute the outer product\\n    scaling_factor = 1.0 / period * 2 * math.pi\\n    sin_input = scaling_factor[None, :] * time[:, None]\\n    return torch.cat([torch.sin(sin_input), torch.cos(sin_input)], dim=1)\\n\\n\\ndef sample_beta(alpha, beta, bsize, device):\\n    alpha_t = torch.as_tensor(alpha, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\n    beta_t = torch.as_tensor(beta, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)\\n    dist = torch.distributions.Beta(alpha_t, beta_t)\\n    return dist.sample((bsize,))\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NKKfyQUCyqntbahrsj5exX","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"class LeRobotLiberoDataConfig(DataConfigFactory):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    This config is used to configure transforms that are applied at various parts of the data pipeline.\\\\n    For your own dataset, you can copy this class and modify the transforms to match your dataset based on the\\\\n    comments below.\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    extra_delta_transform: bool = False\\\\n\\\\n    @override\\\\n    def create(self, assets_dirs: pathlib.Path, model_config: _model.BaseModelConfig) -> DataConfig:\\\\n        # The repack transform is *only* applied to the data coming from the dataset,\\\\n        # and *not* during inference. We can use it to make inputs from the dataset look\\\\n        # as close as possible to those coming from the inference environment (e.g. match the keys).\\\\n        # Below, we match the keys in the dataset (which we defined in the data conversion script) to\\\\n        # the keys we use in our inference pipeline (defined in the inference script for libero).\\\\n        # For your own dataset, first figure out what keys your environment passes to the policy server\\\\n        # and then modify the mappings below so your dataset's keys get matched to those target keys.\\\\n        # The repack transform simply remaps key names here.\\\\n        repack_transform = _transforms.Group(\\\\n            inputs=[\\\\n                _transforms.RepackTransform(\\\\n                    {\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"observation/image\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"image\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"observation/wrist_image\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"wrist_image\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"observation/state\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"state\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"actions\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"actions\\\\\\\",\\\\n                        \\\\\\\"prompt\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"prompt\\\\\\\",\\\\n                    }\\\\n                )\\\\n            ]\\\\n        )\\\\n\\\\n        # The data transforms are applied to the data coming from the dataset *and* during inference.\\\\n        # Below, we define the transforms for data going into the model (``inputs``) and the transforms\\\\n        # for data coming out of the model (``outputs``) (the latter is only used during inference).\\\\n        # We defined these transforms in `libero_policy.py`. You can check the detailed comments there for\\\\n        # how to modify the transforms to match your dataset. Once you created your own transforms, you can\\\\n        # replace the transforms below with your own.\\\\n        data_transforms = _transforms.Group(\\\\n            inputs=[libero_policy.LiberoInputs(model_type=model_config.model_type)],\\\\n            outputs=[libero_policy.LiberoOutputs()],\\\\n        )\\\\n\\\\n        # One additional data transform: pi0 models are trained on delta actions (relative to the first\\\\n        # state in each action chunk). IF your data has ``absolute`` actions (e.g. target joint angles)\\\\n        # you can uncomment the following line to convert the actions to delta actions. The only exception\\\\n        # is for the gripper actions which are always absolute.\\\\n        # In the example below, we would apply the delta conversion to the first 6 actions (joints) and\\\\n        # leave the 7th action (gripper) unchanged, i.e. absolute.\\\\n        # In Libero, the raw actions in the dataset are already delta actions, so we *do not* need to\\\\n        # apply a separate delta conversion (that's why it's commented out). Choose whether to apply this\\\\n        # transform based on whether your dataset uses ``absolute`` or ``delta`` actions out of the box.\\\\n\\\\n        # LIBERO already represents actions as deltas, but we have some old Pi0 checkpoints that are trained with this\\\\n        # extra delta transform.\\\\n        if self.extra_delta_transform:\\\\n            delta_action_mask = _transforms.make_bool_mask(6, -1)\\\\n            data_transforms = data_transforms.push(\\\\n                inputs=[_transforms.DeltaActions(delta_action_mask)],\\\\n                outputs=[_transforms.AbsoluteActions(delta_action_mask)],\\\\n            )\\\\n\\\\n        # Model transforms include things like tokenizing the prompt and action targets\\\\n        # You do not need to change anything here for your own dataset.\\\\n        model_transforms = ModelTransformFactory()(model_config)\\\\n\\\\n        # We return all data transforms for training and inference. No need to change anything here.\\\\n        return dataclasses.replace(\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"36461722-2e57-4b61-892b-43cb09f5000b","uuid":"0a03b18f-63ec-455c-bfae-46c310611bef","timestamp":"2026-05-19T21:15:40.955Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"class LeRobotLiberoDataConfig(DataConfigFactory):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    This config is used to configure transforms that are applied at various parts of the data pipeline.\\\\n    For your own dataset, you can copy this class and modify the transforms to match your dataset based on the\\\\n    comments below.\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    extra_delta_transform: bool = False\\\\n\\\\n    @override\\\\n    def create(self, assets_dirs: pathlib.Path, model_config: _model.BaseModelConfig) -> DataConfig:\\\\n        # The repack transform is *only* applied to the data coming from the dataset,\\\\n        # and *not* during inference. We can use it to make inputs from the dataset look\\n        # as close as possible to those coming from the inference environment (e.g. match the keys).\\n        # Below, we match the keys in the dataset (which we defined in the data conversion script) to\\n        # the keys we use in our inference pipeline (defined in the inference script for libero).\\n        # For your own dataset, first figure out what keys your environment passes to the policy server\\n        # and then modify the mappings below so your dataset's keys get matched to those target keys.\\n        # The repack transform simply remaps key names here.\\n        repack_transform = _transforms.Group(\\n            inputs=[\\n                _transforms.RepackTransform(\\n                    {\\n                        \\\"observation/image\\\": \\\"image\\\",\\n                        \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\": \\\"wrist_image\\\",\\n                        \\\"observation/state\\\": \\\"state\\\",\\n                        \\\"actions\\\": \\\"actions\\\",\\n                        \\\"prompt\\\": \\\"prompt\\\",\\n                    }\\n                )\\n            ]\\n        )\\n\\n        # The data transforms are applied to the data coming from the dataset *and* during inference.\\n        # Below, we define the transforms for data going into the model (``inputs``) and the transforms\\n        # for data coming out of the model (``outputs``) (the latter is only used during inference).\\n        # We defined these transforms in `libero_policy.py`. You can check the detailed comments there for\\n        # how to modify the transforms to match your dataset. Once you created your own transforms, you can\\n        # replace the transforms below with your own.\\n        data_transforms = _transforms.Group(\\n            inputs=[libero_policy.LiberoInputs(model_type=model_config.model_type)],\\n            outputs=[libero_policy.LiberoOutputs()],\\n        )\\n\\n        # One additional data transform: pi0 models are trained on delta actions (relative to the first\\n        # state in each action chunk). IF your data has ``absolute`` actions (e.g. target joint angles)\\n        # you can uncomment the following line to convert the actions to delta actions. The only exception\\n        # is for the gripper actions which are always absolute.\\n        # In the example below, we would apply the delta conversion to the first 6 actions (joints) and\\n        # leave the 7th action (gripper) unchanged, i.e. absolute.\\n        # In Libero, the raw actions in the dataset are already delta actions, so we *do not* need to\\n        # apply a separate delta conversion (that's why it's commented out). Choose whether to apply this\\n        # transform based on whether your dataset uses ``absolute`` or ``delta`` actions out of the box.\\n\\n        # LIBERO already represents actions as deltas, but we have some old Pi0 checkpoints that are trained with this\\n        # extra delta transform.\\n        if self.extra_delta_transform:\\n            delta_action_mask = _transforms.make_bool_mask(6, -1)\\n            data_transforms = data_transforms.push(\\n                inputs=[_transforms.DeltaActions(delta_action_mask)],\\n                outputs=[_transforms.AbsoluteActions(delta_action_mask)],\\n            )\\n\\n        # Model transforms include things like tokenizing the prompt and action targets\\n        # You do not need to change anything here for your own dataset.\\n        model_transforms = ModelTransformFactory()(model_config)\\n\\n        # We return all data transforms for training and inference. No need to change anything here.\\n        return dataclasses.replace(\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01GuMCYj2269ehGMcuKWdBfU","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_019Q73y6wUbYyDu7RCik9D9F","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"westd-35807","command":"cat << 'PYEOF' > /tmp/verify_weights_v2.py\n\"\"\"Verify weight loading for train_baseline.py on cuda:3\"\"\"\nimport os, sys\nos.environ[\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\"] = \"3\"\nos.environ[\"HF_LEROBOT_HOME\"] = \"/root/autodl-tmp/colliforce_data\"\n\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/root/colliforce-vla/openpi\")\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/root/colliforce-vla/openpi/src\")\n\nimport torch\nimport safetensors.torch\n\ndef main():\n    import openpi.models.pi0_config as pi0_config\n    import openpi.models_pytorch.pi0_pytorch as pi0_pytorch\n\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"PART 1: Build model and load weights\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    \n    model_config = pi0_config.Pi0Config(\n        paligemma_variant=\"gemma_2b_lora\",\n        action_expert_variant=\"gemma_300m_lora\",\n    )\n    model = pi0_pytorch.PI0Pytorch(model_config).to(\"cuda:0\")\n    total_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())\n    trainable_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad)\n    print(f\"Total params: {total_params:,}\")\n    print(f\"Trainable params: {trainable_params:,}\")\n    \n    # Load weights\n    weight_path = \"/root/autodl-tmp/openpi_weights/pi0_base\"\n    model_path = os.path.join(weight_path, \"model.safetensors\")\n    \n    test_p = dict(model.named_parameters())['action_in_proj.weight']\n    print(f\"BEFORE load - mean={test_p.data.float().mean():.6f}, std={test_p.data.float().std():.6f}\")\n    \n    missing, unexpected = safetensors.torch.load_model(model, model_path)\n    print(f\"Missing: {len(missing)}, Unexpected: {len(unexpected)}\")\n    \n    test_p = dict(model.named_parameters())['action_in_proj.weight']\n    print(f\"AFTER load - mean={test_p.data.float().mean():.6f}, std={test_p.data.float().std():.6f}\")\n    \n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"PART 2: Data loader with norm_stats\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    \n    import openpi.training.config as _config\n    from openpi.training.optimizer import CosineDecaySchedule\n    import openpi.training.data_loader as _data\n    \n    config = _config.TrainConfig(\n        name=\"baseline_spatial_bowl\",\n        project_name=\"colliforce-vla\",\n        exp_name=\"verify_test\",\n        model=model_config,\n        data=_config.LeRobotLiberoDataConfig(\n            repo_id=\"safelibero_spatial\",\n            base_config=_config.DataConfig(prompt_from_task=True),\n            extra_delta_transform=True,\n        ),\n        pytorch_weight_path=weight_path,\n        freeze_filter=model_config.get_freeze_filter(),\n        ema_decay=None,\n        lr_schedule=CosineDecaySchedule(\n            warmup_steps=1000, peak_lr=2.5e-5,\n            decay_steps=30000, decay_lr=2.5e-6,\n        ),\n        num_train_steps=2,\n        batch_size=2,\n        save_interval=2,\n        log_interval=1,\n        checkpoint_base_dir=\"/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints\",\n        seed=0,\n        wandb_enabled=False,\n        overwrite=True,\n    )\n    \n    # Use num_workers=0 to avoid multiprocessing issues\n    import torch.utils.data\n    loader = _data.create_data_loader(config, framework=\"pytorch\", shuffle=True)\n    \n    # Monkey-patch to use num_workers=0\n    data_config = loader.data_config()\n    if hasattr(data_config, 'norm_stats') and data_config.norm_stats:\n        print(f\"norm_stats keys: {list(data_config.norm_stats.keys())}\")\n    else:\n        print(\"WARNING: norm_stats is None or empty!\")\n    \n    # Get one batch manually\n    dataset = loader.dataset if hasattr(loader, 'dataset') else None\n    print(f\"Loader type: {type(loader)}\")\n    print(f\"Dataset type: {type(dataset) if dataset else 'N/A'}\")\n    \n    # Try to get data\n    import jax\n    \n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"PART 3: Forward pass loss check\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    \n    model.train()\n    \n    for i, batch in enumerate(loader):\n        if i > 0:\n            break\n        observation, actions = batch\n        observation = jax.tree.map(lambda x: x.to(\"cuda:0\"), observation)\n        actions = actions.to(torch.float32).to(\"cuda:0\")\n        print(f\"actions shape: {actions.shape}, range: [{actions.min():.3f}, {actions.max():.3f}]\")\n        print(f\"state shape: {observation.state.shape}\")\n        \n        with torch.no_grad():\n            losses = model(observation, actions)\n            loss = losses.mean()\n            print(f\"\\nLoss with loaded weights: {loss.item():.4f}\")\n            print(f\"Expected ~0.2 (good) or ~100+ (weights not loaded)\")\n    \n    # Random init comparison\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"PART 4: Random init comparison\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    \n    model2 = pi0_pytorch.PI0Pytorch(model_config).to(\"cuda:0\")\n    model2.train()\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        losses2 = model2(observation, actions)\n        loss2 = losses2.mean()\n        print(f\"Random init loss: {loss2.item():.4f}\")\n    \n    del model2\n    torch.cuda.empty_cache()\n    \n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"PART 5: suffix_out shape for SDF integration\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    \n    # Hook to capture suffix_out\n    suffix_out_captured = {}\n    def hook_fn(module, input, output):\n        # action_out_proj receives suffix_out as input\n        suffix_out_captured['value'] = input[0].detach()\n    \n    handle = model.action_out_proj.register_forward_hook(hook_fn)\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        _ = model(observation, actions)\n    handle.remove()\n    \n    if 'value' in suffix_out_captured:\n        so = suffix_out_captured['value']\n        print(f\"suffix_out shape: {so.shape}\")\n        print(f\"suffix_out dtype: {so.dtype}\")\n        print(f\"suffix_out mean: {so.mean():.4f}, std: {so.std():.4f}\")\n        print(f\"Mean-pooled features shape: {so.mean(dim=1).shape}\")\n    \n    del model\n    torch.cuda.empty_cache()\n    \n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"VERIFICATION COMPLETE\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\nPYEOF\necho \"Script written\"","account":"root"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":808,"cache_read_input_tokens":110602,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":808},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"36461722-2e57-4b61-892b-43cb09f5000b","uuid":"e6554a5e-3d92-47ea-95a0-b57f63959da5"}
